&=\|U^{T}R^{T}RA(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}+\frac{1}{3}\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\end{split}

(38)

where the last inequality is because
R
is a
(1\pm\frac{1}{3})

\ell_{2}
subspace embedding for
A
. Rearranging gives

\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|U^{T}R^{T}RA(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}

We bound the right hand side using the inequality
\|U^{T}R^{T}R(AX^{*}-B)\|_{F}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}
(recall that we are conditioning on
\mathcal{E}
). By the normal equations for the problem
\min_{X}\|RAX-RB\|_{F}
,

U^{T}R^{T}R(A\widehat{X}-B)=A^{T}R^{T}R(A\widehat{X}-B)=0

meaning that

U^{T}R^{T}RA(\widehat{X}-X^{*})=U^{T}R^{T}R(A\widehat{X}-B+B-AX^{*})=U^{T}R^{T}R(B-AX^{*})

and therefore, conditioning on
\mathcal{E}
,

\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|U^{T}R^{T}RA(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}=\frac{3}{2}\|U^{T}R^{T}R(B-AX^{*})\|_{F}\leq O(\varepsilon)\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}

Therefore,

\begin{split}\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}^{2}&=\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}^{2}+\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=O(\varepsilon)\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}+\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=(1+O(\varepsilon))\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}\end{split}

(39)

and taking square roots gives

\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{1+O(\varepsilon)}\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the algorithms described below, the previous two lemmas will be used with
t\in O(\frac{k}{\varepsilon})

D.2
FPT Tucker-
(p,q)
Decomposition

We now prove the approximation error and running time guarantees of Algorithm
2
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

We split the analysis of Algorithm
2
into three parts: (1) we show that among the guesses
U^{1},U^{2},\ldots,U^{p}
, there is a good solution for Tucker-
(p,q)
, (2) we then show that, using the Kronecker product regression algorithm of
[
DJS+19
]
(with very minor modifications) we can identify that good solution
U^{1},U^{2},\ldots,U^{p}
, and (3) we analyze the running time.

Part 1: Existence of a Good Guess:

Let
T
be the number of trials per mode in Algorithm
2
— that is, the number of guesses we make for each of
U^{1},U^{2},\ldots,U^{p}
. We will determine how to select a sufficiently large value of
T
at the end of the algorithm. For the purpose of our analysis, for a mode
m\in[p]
, we use the notation
U^{m}_{t}
to refer to the guess for
U^{m}
that is generated on the
t^{th}
trial. Throughout the proof, fix an optimal solution
U^{1}_{*},U^{2}_{*},\ldots,U^{p}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, and
G_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times\ldots\times k\times n\times\ldots\times n}
. We will use an argument reminiscent of the “iterative existential argument” of
[
SWZ19
]
to show that with the desired success probability, there exist
\widehat{U^{1}},\ldots\widehat{U^{p}}
, with
\widehat{U^{1}}
among the guesses for
U^{1}
,
\widehat{U^{2}}
among the guesses for
U^{2}
, and so on, such that

\begin{split}\min_{G}\|(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p}}&\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|(U^{1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

For each mode
m\in[p]
, let
\mathcal{S}_{m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
be the set of all guesses made for
U^{m}
over the course of Algorithm
2
. Define

\begin{split}\widehat{U^{1}}&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\|(U\times U^{2}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\|UM_{1}(G_{*})(U^{2}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}-M_{1}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(40)

For each
m\in[p]
,
m\neq 1,p
, define

\begin{split}\widehat{U^{m}}&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{m}}\|(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{m-1}}\times U\times U^{m+1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{m})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{m}}\|UM_{m}(G_{m})(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{m-1}}\times U^{m+1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(41)

where
G_{m}
is the optimal core tensor corresponding to
\widehat{U^{1}},\ldots,\widehat{U^{m-1}},U^{m}_{*},\ldots,U^{p}_{*}
, and define

\begin{split}\widehat{U^{p}}&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{p}}\|(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p-1}}\times U\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{p})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&=\argmin_{U\in\mathcal{S}_{p}}\|UM_{p}(G_{p})(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p-1}}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}-M_{p}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(42)

For convenience, define
W_{1}=M_{1}(G_{*})(U^{2}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}
, for
m\in[p]
,
m\neq 1,p
, define
W_{m}=M_{m}(G_{m})(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{m-1}}\times U^{m+1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}
, and define
W_{p}=M_{p}(G_{p})(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p-1}}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})^{T}
.

Now, fix a mode
m\in[p]
. First, let
\mathcal{E}^{m}
be the event that the Countsketch matrix
S_{m}
is an affine embedding for
W_{m}
and
M_{m}(A)
, that is, for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,

\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}=\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\|UW_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}

Observe that if
S_{m}
has
O(\frac{p^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}})
rows, then by Theorem
8
,
\mathcal{E}^{m}
holds with probability at least
1-O(1/p)
(note that
S_{m}
is independent of
W_{m}
). Next, let
\mathcal{F}^{m}
be the event that the random sign matrix
T_{m}
preserves
(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}})
-approximate
\ell_{2}
regression solutions to the problem
\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}
. In other words, let
\mathcal{F}^{m}
be the event that for any
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
for which

\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}

it also holds that

\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}

Then, by Lemma
12
(with
\varepsilon
replaced by
\frac{\varepsilon}{p}
,
\tau
replaced by
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}}
, and
\delta=\frac{1}{p}
),
\mathcal{F}^{m}
also holds with probability at least
1-O(1/p)
as long as
T_{m}
has at least
O((pk\log p)/\varepsilon)
rows (note again that
T_{m}
is independent of
S_{m}
and
W_{m}
).

Now, for any
t\in[T]
, let
\mathcal{G}^{m}_{t}
be the event that the Gaussian matrix
R_{m,t}
with
k
rows and i.i.d.
N(0,1/k)
entries, produced on the
t^{th}
trial, obtains a
(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}})
-approximate
\ell_{2}
regression solution to the above problem (note that the Gaussian matrix is denoted
R_{m}
in Algorithm
2
, but for the purposes of the analysis we denote it as
R_{m,t}
). In other words,
\mathcal{G}^{m}_{t}
is the event that if we define

U^{m}_{t}=\argmin_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}\|_{F}=M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}(W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T})^{\dagger}

then

\|U^{m}_{t}W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}

By Lemma
17
, for a fixed
m\in[p]
and
t\in[T]
,
\mathcal{G}^{m}_{t}
holds with probability at least
(\frac{\varepsilon}{pk})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
, where we replaced
\varepsilon
with
\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}}
in the statement of Lemma
17
, and also used the fact that
T_{m}
has
t=O(\frac{pk\log p}{\varepsilon})
rows. (Note that above we have defined
U^{m}_{t}=M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}(W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T})^{\dagger}
, whereas in Algorithm
2
we define
U^{m}_{t}=M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}
. These are essentially equivalent, since the factor
(W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
just amounts to a change of basis — thus, for the purpose of the algorithm, this factor is not needed (note that it anyways cannot be computed efficiently) and for the purpose of the analysis, we can simply redefine
G_{m+1}
later in the analysis, using the value
U^{m}_{t}=M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}
instead of
M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T}(W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}R_{m,t}^{T})^{\dagger}
. Let
f=(\frac{\varepsilon}{pk})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. Observe that if we select
T=O(\frac{p}{f})=(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
, then with probability at least

1-(1-f)^{T}=1-(1-f)^{O(p/f)}\geq 1-e^{-O(p)}

there exists at least one
t\in[T]
such that
\mathcal{G}^{m}_{t}
holds.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{m}
be the event that
\mathcal{G}^{m}_{t}
holds for at least one
t\in[T]
. Now, let
\mathcal{Q}^{m}=\mathcal{E}^{m}\cap\mathcal{F}^{m}\cap\mathcal{G}^{m}
. Observe that
\mathcal{Q}^{m}
holds with probability at least
(1-O(1/p))^{2}(1-e^{-O(p)})\geq(1-O(1/p))
. If
\mathcal{Q}^{m}
holds, then
\mathcal{G}^{m}
holds, meaning that for some
t\in[T]
,

\|U^{m}_{t}W_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\|_{F}

In addition
\mathcal{F}^{m}
holds, meaning that

\|U^{m}_{t}W_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}S_{m}^{T}-M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}\|_{F}

Finally,
\mathcal{E}^{m}
holds, meaning that
S_{m}
is a
(1\pm O(\varepsilon/p))

\ell_{2}
affine embedding for
W_{m}
and
M_{m}(A)
, and

\|U^{m}_{t}W_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}

In summary,
\mathcal{Q}^{m}
holds with probability at least
1-O(1/p)
and implies that

\|U^{m}_{t}W_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}

for some
t\in[T]
— in particular, by the definition of
\widehat{U^{m}}
, this implies that

\begin{split}\|\widehat{U^{m}}W_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UW_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(43)

Finally, observe that by reshaping and induction on
m
, we can show that if
\mathcal{Q}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{Q}^{m-1}
hold, then

\min_{U}\|UW_{m}-M_{m}(A)\|_{F}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)^{m-1}\|(U^{1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}

To see why, note that we can iteratively replace
U^{m}_{*}
with
\widehat{U^{m}}
to lose a
(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{p})
factor in the error (this follows from Equation
43
, which holds when
\mathcal{Q}^{m}
holds for any
m\in[p]
) and replacing
G_{m-1}
with
G_{m}
can only decrease the error. Thus, if
\mathcal{Q}^{1},\ldots\mathcal{Q}^{p}
all hold, then

\begin{split}\|(\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p}}&\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{p})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\Big)\Big)^{p}\|(U^{1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\|(U^{1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(44)

where the second inequality is because
(1+O(\varepsilon/p))^{p}\leq e^{\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\cdot p}=e^{\varepsilon}
, and by the inequality
e^{x}\leq\frac{1}{1-x}
for
x>0
. Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the event that Equation
44
holds — then,
\mathcal{Q}
occurs with probability at least

\mathbf{Pr}\Big[\mathcal{Q}^{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathcal{Q}^{p}\Big]\geq\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{Pr}\Big[\mathcal{Q}^{i}\Big]\geq\Big(1-O\Big(\frac{1}{p}\Big)\Big)^{p}\geq\frac{9}{10}

Here, the last inequality holds as long as
\mathcal{Q}^{m}
occurs with probability at least
1-\frac{C}{p}
for a sufficiently small constant
C
, and this can be achieved by setting the failure probabilities sufficiently small in
\mathcal{E}^{m}
and
\mathcal{F}^{m}
, and multiplying the number of trials
T
by a sufficiently large constant.

Part 2: Selecting the Best (or nearly best) Guess Using Kronecker Product Regression:

To identify
\widehat{U^{1}},\ldots\widehat{U^{p}}
defined above, we try all possible
p
-tuples of guesses. In other words, if for any mode
m\in[p]
,
\mathcal{S}_{m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
is the set of all guesses made by algorithm for
U^{m}
, then we try all
p
-tuples in
\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{S}_{p}
(here the
\times
symbol denotes the Cartesian product). Each
\mathcal{S}_{m}
has
T
elements, meaning that there are overall
T^{p}
guesses — thus, we will instantiate the algorithm of
[
DJS+19
]
(with the parameters changed so that it can output a good estimate of the error, in addition to outputting a near-optimal solution — the changes are described in Theorem
15
) with failure probability
\delta=\frac{1}{CT^{p}}
, where
C
is an approprately large constant.

Note that if we directly apply the algorithm of
[
DJS+19
]
with
U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}\times\ldots I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n}
, the running time of this subroutine will be at least
n^{q-p}
, meaning the overall running time will be at least
n^{q-p}\cdot T^{p}
. Thus, we define the matrix
A_{p}
and use a Countsketch matrix to obtain a PCP of
A_{p}
as defined in Algorithm
2
. Specifically, let
A_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{p}\times n^{q-p}}
so that the entry of
A_{p}
in the index
((i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{p}),(i_{p+1},\ldots,i_{q}))
is
A(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{q})
, as in Algorithm
2
. Then, for any tuple
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
, note that the Kronecker product regression problem

\min_{G}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}

is equivalent to

\min_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{p}\times n^{q-p}}}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}\|_{F}

Observe also that by Theorem
10
, if
S_{\textsc{PROJ}}
is a Countsketch matrix having
O(\frac{k^{2p}}{\varepsilon^{2}})
rows, then with probability at least
\frac{999}{1000}
,
S_{\textsc{PROJ}}
preserves the error when projecting
A_{p}
onto any
k^{p}
-dimensional subspace up to a
(1\pm\varepsilon)
factor, i.e. if
P
is any rank-
k^{p}
projection matrix, then

\|A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}^{T}-PA_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}^{T}\|_{F}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\|A_{p}-PA_{p}\|_{F}

Note that regression problem
\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}\|_{F}
is equivalent to computing the error when projecting
A_{p}
onto the column span of the
k^{p}
-dimensional subspace spanned by
U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}
, and thus

\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}^{T}\|_{F}=(1\pm\varepsilon)\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}\|_{F}

For convenience, let
M=A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}^{T}
. Observe that by Theorem
15
, for each
i\in[O(k^{2p}/\varepsilon^{2})]
, it is possible to obtain an estimate
\widehat{e_{i}}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{X_{i}}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X_{i}-M_{i}\|_{F}^{2}-\|M_{i}\|_{F}^{2}
in
O(nkp)+(k^{p}/(\varepsilon\delta))^{O(1)}=O(nkp)+(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
running time (and failure probability
\delta=\frac{1}{CrT^{p}}
where
r
is the number of rows in the Countsketch matrix
S_{\textsc{PROJ}}
). Since
M
has
O(\frac{k^{2p}}{\varepsilon^{2}})
columns, it is therefore possible to obtain (with probability
1-\frac{1}{CT^{p}}
) an estimate

\widehat{e}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-M\|_{F}^{2}-\|M\|_{F}^{2}

in running time
n\cdot(k^{p}/\varepsilon)^{O(1)}+(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. Now recall that in Algorithm
2
, we separately pre-compute
\|M\|_{F}^{2}=\|A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}\|_{F}^{2}
before evaluating the errors from each of the tuples
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
— thus, we can use
\widehat{e}+\|M\|_{F}^{2}
as our estimate of the error of
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
. Observe that with probability
1-\frac{1}{CT^{p}}
,

\begin{split}\widehat{e}+\|M\|_{F}^{2}&=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{X}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p})X-A_{p}\|_{F}^{2}\\
&=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{G}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}^{2}\end{split}

(45)

where the second equality is by the consequence of Theorem
10
, since
S_{\textsc{PROJ}}
preserves the error when projecting
A_{p}
onto the subspace
U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}
. Thus, with probability
1-\frac{1}{C}
, simultaneously for all tuples
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\ldots\times S_{p}
, if
\widehat{e}
is the error estimate for
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
defined above, then

\widehat{e}+\|M\|_{F}^{2}=(1\pm O(\varepsilon))\min_{G}\|(U^{1}\times\ldots\times U^{p}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}^{2}

In particular, if
(\widetilde{U^{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{U^{p}})
is the
p
-tuple which achieves the smallest value of
\widehat{e}+\|M\|_{F}^{2}
over the course of the algorithm, then

\begin{split}\min_{G}\|(\widetilde{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widetilde{U^{p}}&\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}^{2}\\
&\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\min_{G}\|\widehat{U^{1}}\times\ldots\times\widehat{U^{p}}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G)-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}^{2}\\
&\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\|(U^{1}_{*}\times\ldots\times U^{p}_{*}\times I_{n}\times\ldots\times I_{n})\text{vec}(G_{*})-\text{vec}(A)\|_{F}^{2}\end{split}

(46)

Note that the second inequality is by Equation
44
(note that it holds with probability at least
\frac{9}{10}
as mentioned in the discussion below Equation
44
). In addition, the first inequality holds with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{C}
, where
C
can be chosen to be a large constant.

Part 3: Running Time Analysis:

First let us analyze the running time of generating the guesses for
U^{m}
and forming the sets
\mathcal{S}_{m}
for
m\in[p]
. Let us first calculate the time needed to form
\mathcal{S}_{m}
— then, this running time will be multiplied by
p
. First, we multiply
M_{m}(A)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{q-1}}
on the right by a Countsketch matrix
S_{m}^{T}
, which takes
O(\text{nnz}(A))
time. We then multiply this matrix on the right by a sign matrix
T_{m}
, and this takes
O(n\cdot\frac{pk\log p}{\varepsilon})
. Next, for
T
trials, we create a Gaussian matrix
R_{m,t}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times O(\frac{pk\log p}{\varepsilon}))}
, and then multiply
M_{m}(A)S_{m}^{T}T_{m}^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times O(\frac{pk\log p}{\varepsilon})}
by
R_{m,t}^{T}
— this operation takes
O(\frac{npk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})
time, and over the course of
T
trials takes
O(\frac{npk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}=n\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
time. Thus, the running time needed to form
\mathcal{S}_{m}
for a single
m\in[p]
is
O(\text{nnz}(A))+n\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. The running time needed to form
\mathcal{S}_{m}
for all
m\in[p]
is thus
O(p\cdot\text{nnz}(A))+n\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{pk^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
.

Now, we analyze the running time needed to evaluate the error of all the tuples
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
in
\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\ldots\mathcal{S}_{p}
. The time needed to compute
A_{p}S_{\textsc{PROJ}}^{T}
and initialize
\textsc{MinError}\leftarrow\|A\|_{F}^{2}
is
O(\text{nnz}(A))
. We must also pre-compute
\|M\|_{F}^{2}
, which can be done in
\text{nnz}(M)
time, which is at most
\text{nnz}(A)
time since there are at most
\text{nnz}(A)
nonzero entries of
M
. Now, the number of tuples
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{S}_{p}
is
T^{p}=(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. For each tuple, and for each of the
O(\frac{k^{2p}}{\varepsilon^{2}})
columns
M_{j}
of
M
, we run
\textsc{ErrorEstimate}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p},M_{j},\delta=\frac{1}{1000rT^{p}})
(the Kronecker product regression algorithm of
[
DJS+19
]
), and since the dimension
d
of the regression problem is
k^{p}
, the running time of this step is
\sum_{m=1}^{p}\text{nnz}(U^{m})+(\frac{k^{p}}{\varepsilon\delta})^{O(1)}=O(nkp)+(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. This running time is incurred
T^{p}\cdot O(\frac{k^{2p}}{\varepsilon^{2}})=(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
times, meaning the overall running time needed to find the best (up to a
1\pm\varepsilon
factor) tuple
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{p})
is
n\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. Combining the running times from the first and the second steps, we find that the overall running time is
O(p\cdot\text{nnz}(A))+n\cdot(\frac{pk}{\varepsilon})^{O(\frac{p^{2}k^{2}\log p}{\varepsilon})}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8
.

Note that we can also output a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximate core tensor
G
using the algorithm of
[
DJS+19
]
. In the case
p=q
this will only lead to an additional term of
O(qnk)+k^{O(q)}/\varepsilon^{O(1)}
in the running time. In general the additional running time would be
n^{q-p}\cdot(O(pnk)+k^{O(p)}/\varepsilon^{O(1)})
.

D.3
FPT Tensor Train Decomposition

In this section, we
obtain a fixed-parameter tractable algorithm for Tensor Train decomposition with output rank exactly
k
. Our algorithm is shown in Algorithm
6
, and we give guarantees for this algorithm in Theorem
3
.

Algorithm 6

(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation algorithm for Tensor Train decomposition with output rank exactly
k
and fixed-parameter tractable running time. This algorithm is similar to our bicriteria algorithm for tree networks (specialized to the case of Tensor Train), but uses an exponential number of trials for each new mode (with Gaussian matrices having
k
columns) to ensure that the output rank is exactly
k
.
ApproxBestCandidate
efficiently finds the candidate in
L_{q}
that (approximately) minimizes the Frobenius norm distance to
A
, and is shown in Algorithm
7
.

A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}
with
q
modes,
\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}

U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
U_{2},\ldots,U_{q-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n\times k}
,
U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}

P_{1}\leftarrow
An
r\times n^{q-1}
Countsketch matrix, where
r\in O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
.

L_{1}\leftarrow\{\}
// This will contain the candidates for
U^{1}
.

T\leftarrow\log\Big(\frac{q}{\delta}\Big)\cdot\Big(e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(qk^{2}/\varepsilon\cdot\log(qk/\varepsilon))}\Big)

for

t=1,\ldots,T

do

R_{1,t}\leftarrow
A
m\times r
matrix whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from
\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}
, where
m\in O(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})

R_{1,t}\leftarrow
An
k\times m
matrix whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from
N(0,\frac{1}{k})

\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}\leftarrow M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1}^{T}R_{1}^{T}

L_{1}\leftarrow L_{1}\cup\{\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}\}

end

for

S_{1}\leftarrow
An
s_{1}\times n
Countsketch matrix, where
s_{1}=O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})

for

i=2,\ldots,q-1

do

P_{i}\leftarrow
An
n^{q-i}\times r
Countsketch matrix, where
r=O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})

L_{i}\leftarrow\{\}
// This will contain the candidates for
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i})
.

// Iterate through all the candidates in
L_{i-1}
, and for each one, add
T
candidates for
U^{i}
.

for

(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i-1})\in L_{i-1}

do

for

t=1,\ldots,T

do

Q_{i,t}\leftarrow
A
m\times r
matrix whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from
\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\}
, where

m=O(k^{2}q/\varepsilon)

// Here
\widetilde{U^{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{2}}
is the solution to the regression problem

\min_{U}\|U(M_{i}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{i-1})\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}))(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T}Q_{i}^{T}R_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T}Q_{i}^{T}R_{i}^{T})\|_{F}

// and
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{i-1}\times n^{i-1}}
is as in Definition
20
, specified by the Countsketch matrices

//
S_{1},\ldots,S_{i-1}
.

R_{i,t}\leftarrow
An
k\times m
matrix whose entries are i.i.d.
N(0,\frac{1}{k})

\widetilde{U^{i}}\leftarrow M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})((M_{i}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{i-1})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}\times I_{k})

L_{i}\leftarrow L_{i}\cup\{(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i-1},\widetilde{U^{i}})\}

end

for

end

for

S_{i}\leftarrow
An
s_{i}\times s_{i-1}n
Countsketch matrix, where
s_{i}=O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})

end

for

Algorithm
6

(1+\varepsilon)-
approximation algorithm for Tensor Train decomposition with output rank exactly
k
continued.

// Now we find
U^{q}
for each of the candidates for
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q-1})
.

L_{q}\leftarrow\{\}

for

(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q-1})\in L_{q-1}

do

// Here
\widetilde{U^{q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
is the solution to the regression problem

\min_{U}\|UM_{q}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1})\mathcal{L}_{q-1}^{T}-M_{q}(A)\mathcal{L}_{q-1}^{T}\|_{F}

// which can be solved exactly, and
\widetilde{U^{q}}
has
k
columns since the last mode of
U^{q-1}
has dimension
k
.

\widetilde{U^{q}}\leftarrow M_{q}(A)\mathcal{L}_{q-1}^{T}(M_{q}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1})\mathcal{L}_{q-1}^{T})^{\dagger}

L_{q}\leftarrow L_{q}\cup\{(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q-1},\widetilde{U^{q}})\}

end

for

(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})\leftarrow\textsc{ApproxBestCandidate}(L_{q},A)

return

(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})

Algorithm 7

This is a subroutine in Algorithm
6
, which we call once we have found a list
L_{q}
of
2^{\text{poly}(qk/\varepsilon)}
candidates
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
. We find the candidate
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
which approximately minimizes
\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}
, up to a
(1+\varepsilon)
factor. This is achieved by first compressing
A
with Countsketch matrices with
2^{\text{poly}(qk/\varepsilon)}
rows, which contributes
O(q\cdot\text{nnz}(A))
to the running time of this subroutine, and then applying those same Countsketch matrices to each of the candidates
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
which contributes
|L_{q}|\cdot 2^{\text{poly}(qk/\varepsilon)}\cdot n
to the overall running time. The number of rows in the Countsketch matrices is selected so that they approximately preserve
\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}
for all candidates
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
.
Give a name to this subroutine.

L_{q}
contains elements of the form
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
where
U^{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},U^{2},\ldots,U^{q-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n\times k},U^{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}
,
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}
has
q
modes

Returns a candidate
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})\in L_{q}

// First, we compress
A
using Countsketch matrices.

\widetilde{A}\leftarrow A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}

for

i=1,\ldots,q

do

S_{i}\leftarrow
An
s_{1}\times n
Countsketch matrix if
i=1
, and an
s_{i}\times s_{i-1}n
Countsketch matrix otherwise

where
s_{i}=\text{poly}(qk2^{\text{poly}(qk/\varepsilon)}/\varepsilon)

\widetilde{A}\leftarrow S_{i}\circ\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A})
, where
\mathcal{F}
is defined as in Definition
19
.

end

for

// Next, we evaluate all the candidates. Here,
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})
is as in Definition
20
.

(U^{1}_{best},\ldots,U^{q}_{best})\leftarrow(0,\ldots,0)

\textsc{MinCost}\leftarrow\infty

for

(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})\in L_{q}

do

\textsc{CurrentCost}\leftarrow\|\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q})-\widetilde{A}\|_{F}

if

\textsc{CurrentCost}<\textsc{MinCost}

then

\textsc{MinCost}\leftarrow\textsc{CurrentCost}

(U^{1}_{best},\ldots,U^{q}_{best})\leftarrow(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})

end

if

end

for

return

(U^{1}_{best},\ldots,U^{q}_{best})

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Define

\begin{split}U^{1}_{*},\ldots,U^{q}_{*}:=\argmin_{U^{1},\ldots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(47)

Base Case: Finding Candidates for
U^{1}

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
denote the event that
L_{1}
contains a
\widetilde{U^{1}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that

\begin{split}\min_{U^{2},\ldots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ U^{2}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|U^{1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(48)

We will show that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds with probability at least
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
. Note that we can write

\begin{split}U^{1}_{*}=\argmin_{U}\|U\circ U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}-A\|_{F}=\argmin_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})-M_{1}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(49)

Let us define the following events:

•

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
denote the event that
P_{1}
is a
\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)
affine embedding for the row span of
M_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})
and
M_{1}(A)
, that is, for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,

\begin{split}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}\|_{F}&=\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})-M_{1}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(50)

By Theorem
8
, if we let the number of rows of
P_{1}
be
r=O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
, then
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
.

•

For
t\in[T]
, let
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
denote the event that for all
U
satisfying

\begin{split}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots&\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}\|_{F}\end{split}

(51)

it also holds that

\begin{split}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}&-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}\|_{F}\end{split}

(52)

Applying Theorem
12
with
\delta=\frac{1}{3}
and
\varepsilon
replaced by
O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q})
, we find that
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
holds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
as long as
Q_{1}
has
m=O(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
rows.

•

Finally, for
t\in[T]
, let
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
denote the event that, if we define

\begin{split}\widehat{U^{1}_{t}}&=\argmin_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&=M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T}(M_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T})^{\dagger}\\
&=\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}X\end{split}

(53)

where
X=(M_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, then

\begin{split}\|\widehat{U^{1}_{t}}M_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ&\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}\|_{F}\end{split}

(54)

By Lemma
17
, and since
M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1}^{T}
has rank at most
m
, this holds with probability at least
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk})^{O(km)}=e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk})^{O(qk^{2}/\varepsilon)}
.

Thus, for a fixed
t\in[T]
,
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
holds with probability at least
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk})^{O(qk^{2}/\varepsilon)}
, and the probability that for all
t\in[T]
,
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
fail is therefore at most

\begin{split}\Big(1-e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}\Big)^{O(qk^{2}/\varepsilon)}\Big)^{T}&=\Big(1-\frac{1}{e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(qk^{2}/\varepsilon\cdot\log(qk/\varepsilon))}}\Big)^{T}\\
&\leq e^{-\frac{T}{e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(qk^{2}/\varepsilon\cdot\log(qk/\varepsilon))}}}\end{split}

(55)

Here in the first inequality we used the fact that
1-x\leq e^{-x}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, as long as

\begin{split}T\geq\log\Big(\frac{q}{\delta}\Big)\cdot\Big(e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(qk^{2}/\varepsilon\cdot\log(qk/\varepsilon))}\Big)\end{split}

(56)

with probability at least
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
, there exists some
t\in[T]
such that
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
holds.

Note that if
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds, and
t\in[T]
is such that
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
both hold, then
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds, since if
t\in[T]
is such that
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
hold, and
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds, then

\begin{split}\min_{U^{2},\ldots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}\circ U^{2}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}&\leq\|(\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}X)\circ U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}-A\|_{F}\\
&=\|\widetilde{U^{1}_{t}}XM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})-M_{1}(A)\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|\widehat{U^{1}_{t}}M_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{1}^{T}-M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|UM_{1}(U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})-M_{1}(A)\|_{F}\\
&=\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|U\circ U^{2}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(57)

Here the first inequality is because
X\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. The second inequality is by Equation
50
, since
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds. The third inequality is by Equation
52
, since
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
holds (here note that Equation
51
holds, since Equation
54
holds by our assumption that
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
holds). Finally, the fourth inequality is by Equation
50
(since
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds).

In summary,
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds assuming
\mathcal{E}_{1,a}
holds and
\mathcal{E}_{1,b,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
hold simultaneously for some
t\in[T]
. As shown above, this holds with probability at least
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
, as desired.

Inductive Step: Finding Candidates for
U^{i}
for
i\in[q]\setminus\{1,q\}

In the following, let
C
be an absolute constant that we choose appropriately. For
i\in[q]
, let
\mathcal{E}_{i}
be the event that there exists some
(\widetilde{U^{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{U^{i}})\in L_{i}
such that

\begin{split}\min_{U^{i+1},\ldots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i}}\circ U^{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}&\leq\Big(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\Big)^{i}\min_{U^{1},\ldots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(58)

We wish to show by induction that
\mathcal{E}_{i}
holds with probability at least
1-\frac{Ci\delta}{q}
. Thus, assume
\mathcal{E}_{i-1}
holds with probability at least
1-\frac{C(i-1)\delta}{q}
, that is, there exists
(\widetilde{U^{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\in L_{i-1}
such that

\begin{split}\min_{U^{i},\ldots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ U^{i}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}&\leq\Big(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\Big)^{i-1}\min_{U^{1},\ldots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(59)

For convenience, in the proof of the inductive step we will define

\begin{split}U^{i}_{*},\ldots,U^{q}_{*}:=\argmin_{U^{i},\ldots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ U^{i}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(60)

Note that we are redefining
U^{i}_{*},\ldots,U^{q}_{*}
, compared to our definition in the base case. From this definition, it follows that

\begin{split}U^{i}_{*}&=\argmin_{U}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}))-M_{i}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(61)

We now define the following auxiliary events:

•

Let
\mathcal{E}_{i,a}
denote the event that for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{2}}
,

\begin{split}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ&\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}))(I\times P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&=\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*}))-M_{i}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(62)

Note that the above is equivalent to

\begin{split}\|M_{i+1}(\widetilde{U^{1}}&\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ U)^{T}M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T}-M_{\{1,\ldots,i\}}(A)P_{i}^{T}\|_{F}\\
&=\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|M_{i+1}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ U)^{T}M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})-M_{\{1,\ldots,i\}}(A)\|_{F}\end{split}

(63)

and thus it suffices for
P_{i}
to be a
\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)
affine embedding for the row span of
M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})
and
M_{\{1,\ldots,i\}}(A)
. By Theorem
8
, this holds with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
as long as
P_{i}
has
O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
rows.

•

Let
\mathcal{E}_{i,b}
denote the event that for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{2}}
,

\begin{split}&\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&=\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\end{split}

(64)

We can rewrite this as follows:

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{L}_{i-1}&M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})M_{1}(U\circ U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})(I\times P_{i}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-\mathcal{L}_{i-1}M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&=\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\|M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})M_{1}(U\circ U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})(I\times P_{i}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\end{split}

(65)

where on the left-hand side,
I
now denotes the
n\times n
identity matrix. Thus, it suffices for
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
to be a
\Big(1\pm O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)
affine embedding for the column span of
M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})
and
M_{\{1,\ldots,i-1\}}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T})
. By Lemma
5
, this holds as long as
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
has the
(O(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}),O(\frac{\delta}{q}),2)
JL moment property, and by Lemma
20
, it is sufficient for
S_{j}
to have the
(O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q^{3/2}k}),O(\frac{\delta}{q}),2)
JL moment property for
1\leq j\leq i-1
— this holds as long as
S_{j}
has
O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
rows.

•

Now, for
t\in[T]
, let
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
denote the event that, for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{2}}
satisfying

\begin{split}\|U&(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})\|_{F}\end{split}

(66)

it also holds that

\begin{split}\|U&(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\end{split}

(67)

For our purposes, it suffices for
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
to occur with failure probability
\delta=\frac{1}{3}
. We will apply Theorem
12
, with
S
being replaced by
I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}
:

–

First, we need
I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}
to be a
(1\pm\frac{1}{3})

\ell_{2}
subspace embedding for the row span of
M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{i}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T}
with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
. By Lemma
9
, it suffices for
Q_{i,t}^{T}
to be a
(1\pm\frac{1}{3})

\ell_{2}
subspace embedding for the row span of
M_{i}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T}
. By Lemma
8
, this holds with probability at least
\frac{999}{1000}
as long as
Q_{i,t}
has
O(k)
rows.

–

In addition, we need
I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}
to have the
(O(\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}}),c)
approximate matrix product property for a sufficiently small constant
c
. By Theorem
6
, it suffices for
I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}
to have the
(O(\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}}),c,\ell)
JL moment property for some
\ell\geq 2
. Furthermore, by Lemma
2
, this holds if
Q_{i,t}^{T}
has the
(O(\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}}),c,\ell)
JL moment property, and by Lemma
10
,
Q_{i,t}
has the
(O(\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon}{qk}}),c,\log(1/c))
JL moment property as long as it has
O(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
rows.

–

Finally, we need
E[(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})]
to be the identity matrix.

\begin{split}E[(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t})]&=I\times E[Q_{i,t}^{T}Q_{i,t}]\\
&=I\times I\\
&=I\end{split}

(68)

where the second equality holds if we select the entries of
Q_{i,t}
to be i.i.d., since the entries of
Q_{i,t}
have mean
0
.

Thus, by Theorem
12
, as long as
Q_{i,t}
has
O(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
rows,
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
holds with probability at least
\frac{2}{3}
.

•

Finally, for
t\in[T]
, we define the event
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
as follows. First define

\begin{split}&\widehat{U^{i}_{t}}=\argmin_{U}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&=M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})((M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}\times(M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})^{\dagger})\\
&=M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})((M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}\times X)\end{split}

(69)

where
X:=(M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{T}R_{i,t}^{T})^{\dagger}
is unknown. We then let
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
be the event that

\begin{split}&\|\widehat{U^{i}_{t}}(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}\Big)\Big)\min_{U}\|U(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times M_{1}(U^{i+1}_{*}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}_{*})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})\|_{F}\\
\end{split}

(70)

Lemma 18

(
e^{-\text{poly}(k/\varepsilon)}
Success Probability for Multiple-Response
\ell_{2}
Kronecker Regression)
.

Let
m,n,k,s,t\in N,\varepsilon>0
, and
k,t<n
. Suppose
A\in\mathbb{R}^{mn\times k^{2}}
has rank k and
B\in\mathbb{R}^{mn\times s}
has rank
t
. In addition, suppose that
A
can be written as
A_{1}\times A_{2},A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times k},A_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
. Let
R\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n}
have i.i.d.
N(0,1/k)
entries. If
X^{*}=\argmin\|AX-B\|_{F}^{2}
and
\widehat{X}=\argmin\|(I_{m}\times R)AX-(I_{m}\times R)B\|_{F}^{2}
, then
\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}
with probability at least
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk^{2}})^{O(kt)}

Proof.

As in the proof for the general version Lemma
17
,

\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}^{2}=\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}^{2}+\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}

Again, we aim to bound
\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}
. Let
U\in\mathbb{R}^{mn\times k}
be an orthonormal basis for the column span of
A
. As before, substituting
I\times R
for
S
in Lemma
17
, property
\mathcal{E}
holds with probability at least
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}e^{-k(k+t)}
. First,
I_{m}
is always a subspace embedding for
A_{1}
, and if
R
is a subspace embedding for
A_{2}
,
I\times R
is a subspace embedding for
A
by Lemma
9
. This happens with probability
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}
by Lemma
17
because
A_{2}
has
k
columns. Approximate matrix product result from Lemma
16
also holds given subspace embedding, using the same proof as in Lemma
17
, conditioning on
\|(I\times R)B_{j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\Theta(\frac{\varepsilon}{k})\|B_{j}\|_{2}^{2}
instead. This probability is at least that of
\|SB_{j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\Theta(\frac{\varepsilon}{k})\|B_{j}\|_{2}^{2}
where
S
is a regular Gaussian matrix with entries
N(0,1/k)
, since
I\times R
is a block matrix with nonzero entries drawn i.i.d. from
N(0,\frac{1}{k})
. Using the subspace embedding,

\begin{split}&\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}=\|UU^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&=\|U^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&\leq\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)UU^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}+\|(I-U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)U)U^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&=\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}+\|I-U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)U\|_{2}\|U^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&\leq\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}+\frac{1}{3}\|U^{T}A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&=\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}+\frac{1}{3}\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\\
&\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|U(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\end{split}

(71)

Using the same event
\mathcal{E}
as defined in Lemma
17
, noting that
A
has
k^{2}
columns,
\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)(A\widehat{X}-B)\|^{2}_{F}\leq k\varepsilon\|AX^{*}-B\|^{2}|F
. Using normal equations for
\min_{X}\|(I_{m}\times R)AX-(I_{m}\times R)B\|_{F}
,

U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)(A\widehat{X}-B)=A^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)(A\widehat{X}-B)=0

U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})=U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)(B-AX^{*})

Conditioning on
\mathcal{E}
,

\|A(\widehat{X}-X^{*})\|_{F}\leq\frac{3}{2}\|U^{T}(I_{m}\times R)^{T}(I_{m}\times R)(B-AX^{*})\|_{F}\leq O(k\varepsilon)\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}

And therefore

\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}=(1+O(k\varepsilon))\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}^{2}

Taking the square root and using
\frac{\varepsilon}{k}
for
\varepsilon
in the approximate matrix product result gives

\|A\widehat{X}-B\|_{F}\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\|AX^{*}-B\|_{F}

with probability at least
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{k^{2}})^{O(kt)}

∎

By our definition,
\widehat{U_{t}^{i}}=\argmin\|UA^{\prime}(I\times R^{T}_{i,t})-B^{\prime}(I\times R^{T}_{i,t})\|_{F}
, where
A^{\prime}=(M_{i}(\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})\mathcal{L}^{T}_{i-1}\times M_{1}(U_{*}^{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{*}^{q})P_{i}^{T})(I\times Q_{i,t}^{T})
,
B^{\prime}=M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}^{T}_{i-1}\times P^{T}_{i})(I\times Q^{T}_{i,t})
. Therefore
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
is equivalent to
\|\widehat{U_{t}^{i}}A^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\|_{F}\leq(1+O(\varepsilon/q))\min_{U}\|UA^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\|_{F}
.
A^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{2}\times s_{i-1}m},B^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{i-1}m}
. We can invoke the transpose of
18
and conclude that
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
holds with probability
e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk^{2}})^{O(ks_{i-1}m)}
.

Therefore, for a fixed
t\in[T]
,
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
simultaneously hold with probability

\frac{2}{3}e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{qk^{2}})^{O(ks_{i-1}m)}
. The probability that for all
t\in[T]
,
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
or
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
fails is at most

\begin{split}\left(1-\frac{2}{3}e^{-\Theta(k^{2}\log k)(\varepsilon/qk^{2})^{O(ks_{i-1}m)}}\right)^{T}=\left(1-\frac{1}{\frac{3}{2}e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(ks_{i-1}m\log\frac{qk^{2}}{\varepsilon})}}\right)^{T}\\
\leq e^{-\frac{T}{\frac{3}{2}e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(ks_{i-1}m\cdot\log\frac{qk^{2}}{\varepsilon})}}}\end{split}

(72)

As long as
T\geq\log(\frac{q}{\delta})\cdot\frac{3}{2}e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)+\Theta(s_{i-1}km\cdot\log\frac{qk^{2}}{\varepsilon})}
, with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
, there exists some
t\in[T]
such that
\mathcal{E}_{1,c,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,d,t}
both hold,
m\in O(k^{2}q/\varepsilon)
.
.

Next we will show that, if
\exists t\in[T]
such that
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
,
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
simultaneously hold, in addition to
\mathcal{E}_{i,a}
,
\mathcal{E}_{i,b}
both being satisfied,
\mathcal{E}_{i}
holds. Let
A_{1}=M_{i}(\widetilde{U_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}})
,
A_{2}=M_{1}(U_{*}^{i+1}\circ\cdots U_{*}^{q})
.

\begin{split}&\min_{U^{i+1},\cdots,U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\widetilde{U^{i}}\circ U^{i+1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\leq\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ\widetilde{U^{i}}X^{\prime}\circ U_{*}^{i+1}\circ\cdots U_{*}^{q}-A\|_{F}\\
&=\|\widehat{U_{t}^{i}}\left(A_{1}\times A_{2}\right)-M_{i}(A)\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\|\widehat{U_{t}^{i}}(A_{1}\times A_{2})(I\times P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(I\times P_{i}^{T}))\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\|\widehat{U_{t}^{i}}(A_{1}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{i-1}\times A_{2}P_{i}^{T})-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\min_{U}\|U(A_{1}\mathcal{L}^{T}_{i-1}\times A_{2}P^{T}_{i})-M_{i}(A)(\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T}\times P_{i}^{T})\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\min_{U}\|U(A_{1}\times A_{2})-M_{i}(A)\|_{F}\\
&=(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\min_{U^{i},\cdots,U_{q}}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{i-1}}\circ U^{i}\circ\cdots U^{q}\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\left(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\right)^{i-1}\min_{U^{1}\cdots U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}\|_{F}\\
&\leq\left(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\right)^{i}\min_{U^{1}\cdots U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}\|_{F}\end{split}

(73)

The first inequality is true because
X^{\prime}=(A_{1}\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}X
where
X=(A_{2}P_{i}^{T}Q_{i,t}^{Q}R_{i,t}^{T})^{\dagger}
is in
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. The following two inequalities are due to
\mathcal{E}_{i,a}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i,b}
. The fourth inequality is true when
\mathcal{E}_{i,c,t}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i,d,t}
for the
t
picked (and that this
t
must be picked by Subroutine
7
when it exists due to minimization). The fifth inequality is true when
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
and
P_{i}
are affine embeddings. By Lemma
20
and Lemma
5
,
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
is an affine embedding with the
(\frac{\varepsilon}{q},\frac{\delta}{q},\ell)
JL moment property if
S_{i}
has the
(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2q^{3}}},\frac{\delta}{q},\ell)
JL moment property.
s_{i}=O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
,
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
is an
(1\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{q})
affine embedding with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
.
P_{i}
is a Countsketch
O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
columns, therefore is an
1\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{q}
affine embedding with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
by
8
. By union bound on the failure probabilities of the aforementioned conditions,
\mathcal{E}_{i}
holds with probability
1-O(\frac{\delta}{q})
as desired.

Final Case: Finding
U^{q}
for each candidate
(\widetilde{U^{1}},\cdots,\widetilde{U^{q-1}})

.

By induction, we have proven that there is a candidate such that

\min_{U^{q}}\|\widetilde{U_{1}}\circ\cdots\widetilde{U^{q-1}}\circ U^{q}\|_{F}\leq\left(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\right)^{q-1}\min_{U^{1},\cdots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots U^{q}-A\|_{F}.

By Algorithm
6
, for each candidate tuple
(U^{1},\cdots,U^{q})
, using normal equations,
\widetilde{U^{q}}
is the solution to
\min_{U}\|UM_{q}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1})\mathcal{L}^{T}_{q-1}-M_{q}(A)\mathcal{L}^{T}_{q-1}\|_{F}
. We need to show that

\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1}\circ\widetilde{U^{q}}-M_{q}(A)\|F=(1\pm O(\frac{\varepsilon}{q}))\min_{U}\|UM_{q}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1})-M_{q}(A)\|_{F}.

This holds when
\mathcal{L}_{q-1}
is an affine embedding for
M_{q}(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q-1})
and
M_{q}(A)
with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{q}
by Lemma
20
and Lemma
5
. Therefore, if a good candidate
(\widetilde{U^{1}_{*}},\cdots,\widetilde{U^{q-1}_{*}})
exists in
L_{q-1}
at the end, and the
\mathcal{L}_{q-1}
affine embedding holds, we generate a good candidate in
L_{q}
:

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{U^{1}_{*}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{q}_{*}}-A\|_{F}&\leq\left(1+\frac{C\varepsilon}{q}\right)^{q}\min_{U^{1},\cdots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\\
&=\left(1+O(\varepsilon)\right)\min_{U^{1},\cdots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(74)

Each step of the inductive process fails with probability at most
O(\frac{\delta}{q})
, as shown in each sub-event of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
. By union bound, the overall failure probability is therefore at most
O(\delta)
.

The subroutine Algorithm
7
estimates the error of each candidate tuple in
L_{q}
using the Tensor Train sketch
\mathcal{L}_{q}
. We now determine the required sketch sizes of the
S_{i}
in order to obtain the best candidate
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})
up to a
(1+\varepsilon)
factor, with a failure probability of at most
O(\delta)
. More precisely, we would like the following to hold with probability
1-O(\delta)
:
\mathcal{L}_{q}
preserves
\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-\widetilde{A}\|_{F}
up to a factor of
(1+O(\varepsilon))
, for all candidates
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{q})\in L_{q}
. This means that
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})
should have the
(O(\varepsilon),O(\frac{\delta}{|L_{q}|}),2)
JL moment property. By Lemma
20
, it suffices for each of the
S_{i}
to have the
(O(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{q}}),O(\frac{\delta}{|L_{q}|}),2)
JL moment property. Since
|L_{q}|=T^{q-1}
, this means that it suffices for the
S_{i}
to have
O(\frac{q^{2}}{\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{|L_{q}|}{\delta})=O(\frac{q^{2}T^{q-1}}{\varepsilon\delta})
rows, in order for the candidate
(\widetilde{U^{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{U^{q}})
found by Algorithm
7
to be a
(1+O(\varepsilon))
-approximation with constant probability. In other words,

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{U^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{U^{q}}-A\|_{F}&\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\min_{(U^{1},\cdots,U^{q})\in L_{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\\
&\leq(1+O(\varepsilon))\min_{U^{1},\cdots,U^{q}}\|U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q}-A\|_{F}\end{split}

(75)

Runtime Analysis

Note that in each of the
q
iterations, the CountSketch
P
and linear sketch for tensor,
\mathcal{L}
is only applied once, rather than for each of the
T
trials.

•

Generating
L_{1}
: For each
t
,
\widetilde{U_{t}^{1}}=M_{1}(A)P_{1}^{T}Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T}
.
P_{1}
is only applied to
A
once, so the cost of this operation is
O(\text{nnz}(A))
. Then, applying
Q_{1,t}^{T}R_{1,t}^{T}
to the result takes
mr+km
time for each trial, and the overall runtime of this step is
O(\text{nnz}(A)+T(mr+km))
.

•

Generating
L_{2},\ldots,L_{q-1}
: For a fixed
i
, applying
\mathcal{L}_{i-1}
to
A
takes an additional
O(\text{nnz}(A))
time (since we already have the result of applying
\mathcal{L}_{i-2}
to
A
from the
(i-1)^{th}
iteration). Applying
P_{i}
to the result also takes
O(\text{nnz}(A))
time. For each of
|L_{i-1}|T=T^{i}
trials, applying
Q_{i,t}
,
R_{i,t}
, and
(M_{i}(U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{i-1})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}
to the result takes
n\cdot\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
. Note that by Lemma
19
, computing
(M_{i}(U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{i-1})\mathcal{L}_{i-1}^{T})^{\dagger}
also takes
n\cdot\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
time. Thus, for iterations
2
through
q-1
, this step contributes
O(\text{nnz}(A)+n\cdot\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})\cdot T^{q-1})
to the running time.

•

Generating
L_{q}
: The cost of applying the last mode of
\mathcal{L}_{q}
is
O(\text{nnz}(A))
— this only needs to be applied to
A
once.

•

Generating
L_{q}
: for each candidate, the additional cost of applying the last mode of
\mathcal{L}_{q}
is
O(\text{nnz}(A))
. The cost of other operations is
O(k^{2}s_{q-1})
. The total cost is
O(T^{q-1}(k^{2}s_{q-1})+\text{nnz}(A))

•

Selecting candidate: Computing
\widetilde{A}
is
q\cdot\text{nnz}(A)
time since after each application of
S_{i}
, the number of nonzero entries left is still
\text{nnz}(A)
. For each candidate, calculating
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\cdots,S_{1})(U^{1}\circ\cdots\circ U^{q})
is
O(qsnk(s+k))
time by Lemma
19
. The cost of computing the Frobenius norm of the error is
s_{q}
. The total cost is
O(T^{q}\cdot qsnk(s+k)+q\cdot\text{nnz}(A))
, where
s=O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
.

Taking
T=\log(\frac{q}{\delta})\cdot e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k+\Theta(qk^{2}/\varepsilon\cdot\log(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})))}
,
s_{i}\in O(\frac{q^{4}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
in generation of
L_{i}
,
m\in O(k^{2}q/\varepsilon)
,
r\in O(\frac{q^{3}k^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}\delta})
, we get the overall running time guarantee of
T^{q}\cdot n\cdot\text{poly}(qk/(\varepsilon\delta))+q\cdot\text{nnz}(A)
, which is
O(q\cdot\text{nnz}(A))+n\cdot\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon\delta})\cdot e^{\Theta(\frac{q^{2}k^{2}}{\varepsilon}\log(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon}))}
.

For space complexity, note that an
m\times n
CountSketch matrix can be represented in
O(\log m+\log n)
using hash functions. Fixing
\delta
as a constant,
\mathcal{L}
take
O(q\log\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon}))
space.
P_{i}
take
O(q\log\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})+q^{2}\log n)
space.
Q_{i},R_{i}
take
\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})
space. Computing each
\widetilde{U_{i}}
takes
O(qn\text{nnz}(A)\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon}))
. For finding the best candidate, each
S_{i}
takes
O(\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon})+\log\text{poly}(\frac{qk}{\varepsilon}))
. Computing the cost for each candidate takes
O(qn\text{nnz}(A))
. Organizing the computation using backtrack yields polynomial space overall.

∎

Linear Sketch for Tensor Train

This section contains the tools and proofs of the linear sketch
\mathcal{L}
used in the FPT Tensor Train algorithm
6
.

Definition 19

(Fusing First Two Modes)
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times\ldots\times n_{q}}
be a
q
-mode tensor, where
q\geq 2
. Then, we define
\mathcal{F}(A)\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}n_{2}\times\ldots\times n_{q}}
by

(\mathcal{F}(A))_{(i_{1},i_{2}),i_{3},\ldots,i_{q}}=A_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}}

Note that
\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times\ldots\times n_{q}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}n_{2}\times\ldots\times n_{q}}
is a linear map.

Next, we define
\mathcal{L}_{q}
:

Definition 20

(Linear Sketch for Tensor Train)
.

Let
n,q\in\mathbb{N}
. In addition, suppose
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times n}
, and for
i\in[q]\setminus\{1\}
, suppose
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{i}\times s_{i-1}n}
. Then, we define the linear map
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q}):\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}\to\mathbb{R}^{s_{q}}
as follows. Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}
be a
q
-mode tensor. First, define
T_{1}=S_{1}\circ A\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times n\times\ldots\times n}
(which is a
q
-mode tensor), where
\circ
denotes tensor contraction. Next, for
i\in[q]\setminus\{1\}
, define
T_{i}=S_{i}\circ\mathcal{F}(T_{i-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{i}\times n\times\ldots\times n}
(which is a
(q-i+1)
-mode tensor). Then, we define
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})(A)=T_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{q}}
.

Remark 9

(Restating Definition
20
in terms of Kronecker Products)
.

Note that Definition
20
can also be expressed in the following equivalent way. We can regard
\mathcal{L}_{q}
as a map defined on vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n^{q}}
, i.e.
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q}):\mathbb{R}^{n^{q}}\to\mathbb{R}^{s_{q}}
. Given
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{q}}
, we can define
T_{1}:=(S_{1}\times I_{n^{q-1}})A\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\cdot n^{q-1}}
. Next, for each
i\in[q]\setminus\{2\}
, we can define
T_{i}=(S_{i}\times I_{n^{q-i}})T_{i-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{i}\times n^{q-i}}
. Thus,
T_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{q}}
, and we define
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})(A)=T_{q}
. Note that if we identify
q
-mode tensors
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\ldots\times n}
with their vectorizations
\text{vec}(A)\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{q}}
, then this definition of
\mathcal{L}_{q}
is equivalent to Definition
20
.

Definition
20
is useful for showing that
\mathcal{L}_{q}
can be applied to tensors of the form
U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}
very efficiently:

Lemma 19
.

Let
k,q,n_{1},\ldots,n_{q}\in\mathbb{N}
, and suppose
U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times k}
,
U_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n_{j}\times k}
for
j\in[q]\setminus\{1,q\}
, and
U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n_{q}}
. In addition, suppose
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times n_{1}}
, and
S_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times s_{j-1}n_{j}}
for
j\in[q]\setminus\{1\}
. Then,
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})(U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q})
can be computed in
O(qsnk\cdot(s+k))
time, where
n=\max_{j\in[q]}n_{j}
and
s=\max_{i\in[q]}s_{i}
.

Proof.

We use Definition
20
. First note that
T_{1}=S_{1}\circ(U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q})
is simply
(S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}
, and
S_{1}U_{1}
can be computed in
O(s_{1}n_{1}k)
time. Next, to compute
T_{2}
, we must first compute
\mathcal{F}(T_{1})
. However,

\mathcal{F}(T_{1})=\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})\circ U_{3}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}

meaning that to (implicitly) compute
\mathcal{F}(T_{1})
, it suffices to compute
\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})
, and computing
\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})
takes
O(s_{1}n_{2}k^{2})
time since
S_{1}U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times k}
and
U_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n_{2}\times k}
. In addition,

T_{2}=S_{2}\circ\mathcal{F}(T_{1})=S_{2}\circ\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})\circ U_{3}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}

meaning that to compute
T_{2}
(implicitly) it suffices to compute
S_{2}\circ\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})
— since
S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{2}\times s_{1}n_{2}}
and
(S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times n_{2}\times k}
(meaning
\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}n_{2}\times k}
), computing
S_{2}\circ\mathcal{F}((S_{1}U_{1})\circ U_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{2}\times k}
takes
O(s_{2}s_{1}n_{2}k)
time.

In general, we can proceed inductively as follows. Suppose we have computed
T_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times n\times\ldots\times n}
(where
T_{j}
has
(q-j+1)
modes) which can be written as

T_{j}=M_{j}\circ U_{j+1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}

where
M_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times k}
. Then, let us determine the running time needed to compute
T_{j+1}
given
T_{j}
. Note that

T_{j+1}=S_{j+1}\circ\mathcal{F}(T_{j})=S_{j+1}\circ\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})\circ U_{j+2}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}

Thus, if we define
M_{j+1}=S_{j+1}\circ\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j+1}\times k}
, then

T_{j+1}=M_{j+1}\circ U_{j+2}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q}

and therefore, to compute
T_{j+1}
, it suffices to compute
M_{j+1}
. Since
M_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times k}
and
U_{j+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n_{j+1}\times k}
, computing
M_{j}\circ U_{j+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times n_{j+1}\times k}
takes
O(s_{j}n_{j+1}k^{2})
time. Moreover, since
S_{j+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j+1}\times s_{j}n_{j+1}}
and
\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}n_{j+1}\times k}
, computing
M_{j+1}=S_{j+1}\circ\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})
takes
O(s_{j+1}s_{j}n_{j+1}k)
time (once
\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})
has already been computed).

In summary, the overall running time needed to compute
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})(U_{1}\circ\cdots\circ U_{q})
is therefore

\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}O(s_{j}n_{j+1}k^{2})+\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}O(s_{j+1}s_{j}n_{j+1}k)+O(s_{1}n_{1}k)

Here, the first sum comes from the computation of
\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})
for
j\in[q-1]
, and the second sum is the additional cost of computing
S_{j+1}\circ\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})
for
j\in[q-1]
(once
\mathcal{F}(M_{j}\circ U_{j+1})
has already been computed). If we let
s=\max_{j\in[q]}s_{j}
and
n=\max_{j\in[q]}n_{j}
, then the overall running time is therefore
O(q\cdot(snk^{2}+s^{2}nk))
.
∎

The equivalent definition in Remark
9
in terms of Kronecker products is useful for showing that
\mathcal{L}_{q}
has the JL moment property if the
S_{i}
are chosen properly — in fact, it suffices for the
S_{i}
to have the JL moment property, since we can then apply Lemma
2
to conclude that
S_{i}\times I_{n^{q-i}}
has the JL moment property, and since
\mathcal{L}_{q}
is the composition of the maps
S_{i}\times I_{n^{q-i}}
for
i\in[q]
, we can apply Lemma
3
to conclude that
\mathcal{L}_{q}
itself has the JL moment property. We make this precise below:

Lemma 20
.

Suppose that
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times n}
, and for
i\in[q]\setminus\{1\}
,
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{i}\times s_{i-1}n}
, and
S_{1},\ldots,S_{q}
are independent. Moreover, suppose
S_{i}
has the
(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2q}},\delta,\ell)
JL moment property. Then,
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})
has the
(\varepsilon,\delta,\ell)
JL moment property.

Proof.

By Remark
9
, we can write
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q}):\mathbb{R}^{n^{q}}\to\mathbb{R}^{s_{q}}
as

\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})=S_{q}(S_{q-1}\times I_{n})\ldots(S_{2}\times I_{n^{q-2}})(S_{1}\times I_{n^{q-1}})

The factors in the product on the right hand side are independent, and since
S_{i}
has the
(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2q}},\delta,\ell)
JL moment property, by Lemma
2
,
(S_{i}\times I_{n^{q-i}})
also has the
(\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{2q}},\delta,\ell)
JL moment property. By Lemma
3
, their product
\mathcal{L}_{q}(S_{1},\ldots,S_{q})
has the
(\varepsilon,\delta,\ell)
JL moment property. This proves the lemma.
∎

D.4
FPT Bicriteria CP Decomposition

In this section, we give an algorithm for q-mode CP decomposition which outputs
U_{1},\cdots,U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{q-1}}
that obtain a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation factor under the Frobenius norm. Formally, the algorithm solves the following problem:

Problem 4

(
q
-mode CP rank-
k
Decomposition)
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
be a
q
-mode tensor, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We wish to find a
q
-mode tensor
B\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
for which
\lVert A-B\rVert_{F}^{2}
is as small as possible, such that
B
has tensor rank at most
k
. In other words, we would like to find matrices
U_{1},\cdots,U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
that minimizes

\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}{U_{1}}_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes{U_{q}}_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}

Definition 21

(Flattening of
q
-mode tensors)
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
be a
q
-mode tensor, flattening
A
along the each mode respectively yields matrices
A_{1},\cdots,A_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{q-1}}
such that
\forall j_{1},\cdots,j_{q}\in[n],A_{j_{1},\cdots,j_{q}}={A_{i}}_{j_{i},M_{i}}
, where
M_{i}=\sum_{l\in[q],l\neq i,r=q-2}^{r=0}n^{r}j_{l}
. We refer to the inverse of a flatten operation as a re-tensorization.

Algorithm 8

(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation bicriteria rank
k^{q-1}
decomposition with
(q(\text{nnz}(A))+n\text{poly}(k,q/\varepsilon))^{\text{poly}(k,q/\varepsilon)}

A
q-
mode tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\ n},k\in\mathbb{N}

\widehat{U_{1}},\cdots,\widehat{U_{q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{q-1}}

\varepsilon^{\prime}\leftarrow\varepsilon/(q^{3/2})

Generate sketching matrices
S_{1},\cdots S_{q-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{q-1}\times k}
by composing CountSketch matrices of size
n^{(q-1)}\times O(k^{2}/\varepsilon^{\prime 2})
and Gaussian matrices of size
O(k^{2}/\varepsilon^{\prime 2})\times k

Generate CountSketch matrices
T_{1},\cdots,T_{q-1}
of rank
O(k^{2}/\varepsilon^{2})
to implement TensorSketch

\widehat{U_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{q-1}}\leftarrow
matrices with
(A_{i}S_{i})_{j_{i}}
as columns
(j_{1},\cdots,j_{q-1})
for
i\in[q-1]
.

A_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{q-1}}\leftarrow
flattening of
A
along the
q^{th}
mode as in
21

Z_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{q-1}\times n^{q-1}}\leftarrow

\vec{(}(A_{j}S_{j})_{i_{j}})\otimes\cdots\otimes(A_{q-1}S_{q-1})_{i_{q-1}})
as the
M_{i}^{th}
row, where
M_{i}=\sum_{j=1,r=q-2}^{j=q-1,r=0}(i_{j}-1)^{r}+1
.

Solve for minimizer
\widehat{U_{q}}
for
\min_{U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{q-1}}}\lVert YZ_{q}-A_{q}\rVert_{F}^{2}
with
(q-1)
-mode TensorSketch, sketching each face and constructing
Z_{q}
recursively.

return

\widehat{U_{1}},\cdots\widehat{U_{q}}

Algorithm 9

(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation rank-
k
decomposition with
(q\cdot\text{poly}(k/\varepsilon)\cdot\text{nnz}(A))^{\text{poly}(k,q)}
runtime and constant success probability.

A
q
-mode tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}

U_{1},\cdots,U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}

\varepsilon^{\prime}\leftarrow\varepsilon/(2q)

Generate sketching matrices
S_{1},\cdots,S_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{q-1}\times k}
by composing CountSketch matrices of size
n^{(q-1)}\times O(k^{2}/\varepsilon^{\prime 2})
and Gaussian matrices of size
O(k^{2}/\varepsilon^{\prime 2})\times k
.

A_{1},\cdots,A_{q}\leftarrow
flattenings of
A
along each mode as in Definition
21
.

Compute
A_{i}S_{i}
for each
i\in[q]

A_{0}^{\prime}\leftarrow A

for

i=1\rightarrow q

do

{A_{f}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{i-1}n^{q-i}}\leftarrow
flattening of
A_{i-1}^{\prime}
along mode
i
.

{A_{f}^{\prime}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k^{i-1}n^{q-i}}\leftarrow(A_{i}S_{i})^{T}{A_{f}}_{i}

A^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k\times\cdots n\times\cdots\times n}\leftarrow
retensorize
({A_{f}^{\prime}}_{i})
along the
i^{th}
mode. There are
i
dimensions of size
k
and
q-i
dimensions of size
n
.

end

for

A^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times\cdots\times k}\leftarrow A_{q}^{\prime}
, a
q-
mode tensor.

\widehat{X_{1}},\cdots\widehat{X_{q}\leftarrow}

\argmin\lVert X_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{q}-A^{\prime}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, where
X_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},A^{\prime}
is a
q-
mode tensor in
\mathbb{R}^{k\times\cdots\times k}
.

return

A_{1}S_{1}\widehat{X_{1}},\cdots,A_{q}S_{q}\widehat{X_{q}}

Lemma 21
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
,
S_{i}
be the sketching matrices defined in Algorithm
8
. Problem
4
could be approximated by finding
X_{1},\cdots,X_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
that minimize

\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}(A_{1}S_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(A_{q}S_{q}X_{q})_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}

Additionally, there exists
X_{1},\cdots,X_{q}
such that the optimum is at most
(1+\varepsilon)=(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{q}
the optimal solution for Problem
4
.

Proof.

The proof is based on the proof for Theorem C.1. in
[
SWZ19
]
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
be a
q-
mode tensor,
U_{1}^{*},\cdots,U_{q}^{*}=\argmin_{U_{i}}\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bigotimes_{j\in[q]}{U_{j}}_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}
.
Let matrices
Z_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{(q-1)}}
have rows
\text{vec}\left(\bigotimes_{j\in[q],j\neq i}{U_{j}^{*}}_{i}\right)
.

Consider the optimization problem

\min_{U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}{U_{1}}_{i}\otimes{U_{2}^{*}}_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes{U_{q}^{*}}_{i}\rVert_{F}^{2}

By definition of squared Frobenius norm, an equivalent objective function with the same optimum as the original problem is

\min_{U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\lVert U_{1}Z_{1}-A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

Applying the matrix
S_{1}^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{q-1}}
yields the sketched optimization problem

\min_{U_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\lVert U_{1}Z_{1}S_{1}-A_{1}S_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

By Lemma
17
, letting
\widehat{U}_{1}=A_{1}S_{1}(Z_{1}S_{1})^{\dagger}
gives

\lVert\widehat{U_{1}}Z_{1}-A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathcal{OPT}
with probability
e^{\Theta(k^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{kq})^{O(k^{2})}
.
Retensorizing along the first mode gives

\lVert\widehat{U_{1}}\otimes U_{2}^{*}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{q}^{*}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathcal{OPT}

To repeat the process above, in the
i^{th}
round, we fix
\widehat{U}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{U}_{i-1},U^{*}_{i+1},\cdots,U^{*}_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and use the sketching matrix
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{q-1}\times k}
. Let
\widehat{U_{i}}=A_{i}S_{i}(Z_{i}S_{i})^{\dagger}
. We get

\lVert\widehat{U}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes U^{*}_{i+1}\otimes\cdots U^{*}_{q}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{i}\mathcal{OPT}

At the end of the
q^{th}
round, we have

\lVert\bigotimes_{j\in[q]}\widehat{U}_{j}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{q}\mathcal{OPT}

with probability
e^{\Theta(qk^{2}\log k)}(\frac{\varepsilon}{kq})^{O(qk^{2})}
.

Replacing
\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{\prime}\cdot(2q)
, we get

\lVert\bigotimes_{j\in[q]}\widehat{U}_{j}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{OPT}

Note that all
\widehat{U}_{j}
are in the column spans of

A_{j}S_{j}
. i.e.
\widehat{U}_{j}=A_{j}S_{j}X_{j}
for some unknown
X_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 22

(Input Sparsity Reduction)
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
be a
q-
mode tensor. Let
V_{j}
be matrices of size
n\times k
for
j\in[q]
. For every optimization problem

\min\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}(V_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}

with minimizers
X_{1}^{*},\cdots,X_{q}^{*}
, there exists
B\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times\cdots\times k}
a
q-
mode tensor such that

X_{1}^{*},\cdots,X_{q}^{*}=\argmin_{X_{j},j\in[q]}\lVert X_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{q}-B\rVert_{F}^{2}

Proof.

Note that one could write
V_{j}=V_{j}^{\prime}Y_{j}
where the columns of
V_{j}^{\prime}
are orthonormal bases of the column span of
V_{j}
, and that
Y_{j}
could be folded into the unknown
X_{j}
. Without losing generality, consider the case where
V_{j}
’s have orthonormal columns.

Let matrices
Z_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{(q-1)}}
have rows
\text{vec}\left(\bigotimes_{j\in[q],j<i}X_{j}\otimes\bigotimes_{j\in[q],j>i}V_{j}X_{j}\right)
. Let
A_{j}
be the flattened
A
along the
j^{th}
mode as in definition
21
.

\displaystyle\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}(V_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert V_{1}X_{1}Z_{1}-A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert(V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-I_{n})A_{1}+V_{1}(X_{1}Z_{1}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1})\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert V_{1}(X_{1}Z_{1}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1})\rVert_{F}^{2}+\lVert(V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-I_{n})A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle V_{1}V_{1}^{T}\perp V_{1}

\displaystyle=\lVert X_{1}Z_{1}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\lVert(V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-I_{n})A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

The last line is true since
V_{1}
has orthonormal columns. Note that
(V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-I_{n})A_{1}
is a constant term. Therefore, minimizing
\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}(V_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-A\rVert_{F}^{2}
is equivalent to minimizing
\lVert X_{1}Z_{1}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}{X_{1}}_{i}\otimes(V_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

Repeat the process for
j=2
by flattening
\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}{X_{1}}_{i}\otimes(V_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-V_{1}^{T}A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}
along the second mode, folding over
V_{2}^{T}
, and retensorizing again. Iterate up to
j=q
. With the same argument, one could inductively prove that after the
j^{th}
iteration, we arrive at an equivalent form of the problem

\min_{X_{j}}\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}{X_{1}}_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes{X_{j}}_{i}\otimes(V_{j}X_{j})_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes(V_{q}X_{q})_{i}-A^{(j)}\rVert_{F}^{2}

where
A^{(0)}=A,A^{(j)}=\text{Retensorize}(V_{j}^{T}(A^{(j-1)})_{j})
, where
A^{(j-1)})_{j}
is the flattening along the
j^{th}
mode of
A^{(j-1)}
.

After the
q^{th}
iteration, we prove the desired equivalence.
∎

Theorem 20

(Bicriteria
k^{q-1}
-Rank Approximation for CP Decomposition)
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\cdots\times n}
be a
q-
mode tensor. Algorithm
9
finds a
(1+\varepsilon)
-approximation in

(q(\text{nnz}(A))+n\text{poly}(k,q/\varepsilon))^{\text{poly}(k,q/\varepsilon)}
with constant probability.

Proof.

By Lemma
21
, for
\varepsilon^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}

\min_{X_{1},\cdots,X_{q}}\lVert\sum_{l=1}^{k}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{q}(A_{j}S_{j}X_{j})_{l}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{q}\mathcal{OPT}\leq(1+2q\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathcal{OPT}

Expanding the tensor products, this is equivalent to

\min_{X_{1},\cdots,X_{q}}\lVert\sum_{i_{1},\cdots i_{q}=1}^{k}\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{q-1}(A_{j}S_{j})_{i_{j}}\right)\otimes(\sum_{l=1}^{k}(A_{q}S_{q}X_{q})_{l}(X_{1})_{i,l}\cdots(X_{q-1})_{i,l})-A\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+2q\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathcal{OPT}

Let
Z_{q}
be as defined in Algorithm
8
.
Z_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{q-1}\times n^{q-1}}
. Note that
Z_{q}
is the matricization of the tensor product of the first
q-1
sketched faces. As all the
X_{j}
’s are folded into

\sum_{l=1}^{k}(A_{q}S_{q}X_{q})_{l}(X_{1})_{i,l}\cdots(X_{q-1})_{i,l}

, we arrive at an equivalent standard tensor regression problem with
\widehat{U_{1}},\cdots,\widehat{U_{q-1}}
be known, as specified in Algorithm
8
, and
U_{q}
being unknown. Since flattening does not affect squared Frobenius norm due to symmatry, we have

\min_{U_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k^{q-1}}}\lVert U_{q}Z_{q}-A_{q}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq(1+2q\varepsilon^{\prime})\mathcal{OPT}

where
Z_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{k^{q-1}\times n^{q-1}},A_{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{q-1}}
.
However, explicitly solving for this problem introduced a runtime in
\Omega(n^{q-1})
. Therefore, we generate sketching matrices
T_{1},\cdots,T_{q-1}
, sketch each
A_{j}S_{j}
in advance, and construct sketched
Z_{q}
and
A_{q}(T_{1},\cdots,T_{q-1},I)
iteratively as in
[
AKK+20
]
, achieving
O(\text{nnz}(A)\text{poly}(k/\varepsilon))^{q}
cost for computing the sketch and solving the sketched problem, with an additional
\sqrt{q}
factor on the error term. Boosting the success probability of Lemma
21
by repeating the sketch and taking the minimum error yields the desired runtime and success probability.
∎

Remark 10

(rank-
k
Approximation for CP Decomposition)
.

Algorithm
9
outputs
\widehat{U_{1}},\cdots,\widehat{U_{q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that
